Expectations of both augmentation and automation

due to AI are highest among younger and highly-
educated respondents, the same groups that are also
more exposed to the technology in their daily lives and
work. Some 43% of Gen Z expect AI to replace their job
either entirely or in significant part, compared with just
14% of Boomers. At the same time, 34% of Gen Z expect
AI mainly to assist them at work, more than twice the
share among Boomers (15%), while only 11% say they do
not know what AI will mean for their job, compared with

34% of Boomers. A similar pattern emerges by education.
Almost one-third of tertiary-educated respondents
expect full or significant replacement (32%), compared
with 28% among those with primary education, but
they are also almost three times as likely to expect
augmentation (33% versus 12%) and far less likely to be
uncertain (14% versus 36%). Income differences point
in the same direction: high-income respondents are
somewhat more likely than low-income respondents to

expect full or significant replacement (34% versus 29%),
but also more likely to expect AI to assist them (26% versus
17%), while uncertainty is much higher among low-income
respondents (29% versus 17%). The divide is therefore not
simply between groups that expect disruption and those
that do not: the groups most exposed to AI tend to expect
more change in both directions, while less exposed groups
are much more uncertain about what the technology will

mean for them.

### Page 17

14 October 2026
17
To assess whether direct exposure to AI changes
how people expect it to affect their jobs, we split
respondents into three mutually exclusive groups
according to how they currently use the technology.
Around one-third already use AI at work (33%),
comprising both those who use it mainly at work
and equally at work and at home, while 37% use it
only privately and 30% do not use AI at all. We then
compare expectations for AI’s impact on their own

job across these three groups, using the full sample
as a benchmark. This allows us to distinguish whether
greater exposure is associated primarily with fears of
replacement, expectations of augmentation or simply a
clearer view of how work is likely to change.
The more people use AI at work, the more job
transformation they expect, but exposure raises
expectations of augmentation even more strongly
than fears of replacement (Figure 11). Among AI users

at work, 40% expect AI to replace their job entirely or
take over significant parts of it, compared with just 17%
of non-users. However, the gap is even larger on the
positive side: 41% of workplace users expect AI mainly to
assist and support them, almost four times the 11% share
among non-users. This asymmetry fits with our earlier
finding that people who already use AI tend to view the
technology more positively. Exposure appears to make
both the disruptive and complementary potential of AI

more tangible, but it is especially the expected upside
that is missing among non-users.
The other striking gradient is uncertainty, which falls
sharply as exposure to AI increases. Only 11% expect
augmentation, while 33% foresee little or no impact and
another 39% simply do not know what AI will mean for
their job. Together, almost three-quarters of non-users
therefore either expect no meaningful effect or cannot yet
assess one, compared with just 19% of workplace users.

Private-only users again occupy the middle ground: 27%
expect full or significant replacement, 26% augmentation,
30% little or no impact and 18% remain uncertain. The
gradient suggests that direct experience shifts perceptions
away from indifference and uncertainty towards a
much more concrete view of AI as both a source of task
substitution and a tool for productive support. AI users
have thus a much clearer view of how the technology
could change their work, seeing both greater disruption

and substantially greater potential for augmentation.

Allianz Research
AI is likely to reshape far more jobs than it destroys, but the scale of labor-market adjustment will still be
substantial. Our estimates suggest that AI could affect 23% of jobs across the major advanced economies over the next
one to three years³.  The estimate is built bottom-up from more than 800 occupations, which we classify according to
four possible AI impacts: no exposure, automation, augmentation or reorganization. We aggregate these occupation-

level effects into sectoral AI-exposure scores and then to the country level, taking into account sectoral employment
shares as well as structural factors such as AI adoption and preparedness, labor-market regulation and unit labor
costs. Job reorganization is the largest channel, accounting for 10% of jobs, as workers remain in their roles but AI takes
over or changes some of their tasks. A further 8% of jobs face a higher risk of automation, while 5% could benefit from

augmentation. Exposure across countries differs markedly, largely because of differences in their sectoral composition
as well as structural factors, such as labor market regulations and the institutional context. In the US, where finance,
ICT and other knowledge-intensive services account for a larger share of employment, 29% of jobs could be affected,
compared with 15% across the major European economies. Within Europe, the share ranges from 18% in the UK and 16%

in Germany to 14% in France, 12% in Spain and just 9% in Italy. The US therefore faces greater near-term disruption, but is
also better-positioned to capture the productivity and employment gains from AI.
The first effects are likely to appear in hiring and job composition rather than in a sudden rise in aggregate
unemployment. Early evidence already points in this direction: more AI-intensive sectors in the Eurozone have seen
weaker employment growth since late 2022, while younger and less-experienced white-collar workers in routine

cognitive occupations appear particularly exposed. AI may therefore initially show up through fewer entry-level
positions, slower hiring and weaker wage growth in exposed occupations rather than large-scale layoffs. The main
medium-term risk is a transition gap: firms can reorganize and automate tasks faster than workers can move into newly
created roles. Around 25mn jobs across the US and the major European economies are estimated to be subject to

automation risk, while new AI-related employment will only partly offset displacement in the near and medium term.
Over the longer term, we expect both substantial productivity gains and a moderately negative impact on
employment, but the gains are likely to be larger in the US than in Europe (Figure 12). Our ten-year estimates assume
that the AI transition follows a so-called J-curve, with investments and costs upfront, while the gains will only be realized

later⁴. AI-related investment already adds around 0.4pp to US GDP growth in 2026, but the immediate productivity gains
are limited as they get absorbed by reorganization costs and labor-market adjustment. By 2030, US GDP is projected
to grow  +0.6% faster annually, while employment will be 1.5% lower relative to a world without AI. Thereafter, the
productivity dividend becomes more visible: by 2036, cumulative labor productivity is around +4.8% above the no-AI

baseline in the US, while the employment effect largely unwinds to -0.1%, equivalent to roughly -170,000 jobs. In Europe,
productivity gains are more muted and employment effects more persistent: employment remains below the no-AI
baseline across the major economies, reaching around -2.9% in Spain (-650,000 jobs) and -3.1% in Italy (-740,000 jobs),
while Italy records the smallest productivity gain at around +1.8%. Germany and France also remain below baseline

on employment, although by less than Spain and Italy. However, these employment losses will unfold against a rapidly
shrinking workforce in the ageing European societies: by 2036, labor supply is set to decline by 5.6mn workers in
Germany, 4.2mn in Italy and 2.3mn in Spain. Europe therefore faces a double labor-market challenge from demographic
change and AI. The silver lining is that AI may partly compensate for a shrinking workforce in aggregate, but not

one-for-one, because AI-related displacement will not necessarily occur in the same occupations, sectors and regions
where labor shortages emerge. Overall, the US combines the highest near-term AI exposure with the smallest long-run
employment loss, reflecting faster adoption, stronger investment and more effective reallocation. Europe, by contrast,
faces a more persistent employment drag and smaller productivity gains, but part of the AI-related displacement could

help cushion the effects of ageing.
Box 2: AI and the labor market: Disruption first, productivity gains later

The preparedness gap is starkest in training:
adaptation is reaching those already ahead faster
than those most in need of it. Among employed

respondents, only 17% have received AI training at
work, while 44% say their employer offers none (Figure
13). Participation reaches 30% among employed
Americans but just 10% among Poles, and is markedly
higher for men than women, at 21% against 14%. The
education gradient is steeper still, with tertiary-educated
employees roughly three times as likely to have received
training as the secondary-educated. The generational
pattern is more nuanced: Millennials, rather than Gen

Z, record the highest training participation, promoter
share and productivity expectations, while Gen Z is more
likely to be offered training but decline it. The result is
a preparedness gap between anticipated disruption
and actual investment in adaptation. Left unchecked,
AI diffusion risks reinforcing existing advantages rather
than broadening opportunity.
Two demographic patterns sharpen that preparedness
challenge. The first is that the gender divide is often one

of uncertainty rather than outright opinion. Across multiple
questions, women are substantially more likely than men
to answer “don’t know”, with the gap reaching 25% versus
14% on AI’s effect on inequality and 28% versus 20% on
whether their own job will change. Women are therefore
not consistently more hostile to AI; they are less certain
about its implications. The second is a recurring U-shape
by financial strain. Both the financially comfortable and

the most strained tend to be more engaged and more
polarized than those in the middle, whether in their overall
attitudes, expected job disruption or willingness to retrain.
Even among respondents who find it very easy to make
ends meet, a majority still see AI as negative for jobs.
Labor-market anxiety is therefore not confined to the
economically vulnerable.
Preparedness: so far training
reaches mainly those already
ahead

### Page 21

14 October 2026
21
Beyond their own jobs, people expect AI to widen the
gap between winners and losers (Figure 14). Across
the sample, 37% expect AI to increase inequality in
their country, against just 9% who expect it to narrow,
for a net of +28%. This concern is shared across all
countries, from Austria (+40%) to Poland (+20%). The
fear is not simply that AI will replace jobs, but that its
benefits will accrue to some while others bear the costs.

Together with widespread concerns about labor-market
disruption, these findings point to a broader anxiety
about who will benefit from the AI revolution. The
challenge is therefore not only to unlock AI‘s productivity
potential but also to ensure that its gains are widely
shared.
For most people, AI‘s biggest risks are not hypothetical
future scenarios but immediate threats to information,
privacy and jobs. Misinformation and information
manipulation top the list of concerns, cited by 25% of

respondents, followed by data privacy and misuse of
personal information (21%) and job losses (17%) (Figure
15). The pattern is remarkably consistent across countries,
from Austria to the US. The loss of human control over
advanced AI systems, a prominent concern in the AI-safety
debate, ranks fourth at 16%, while the concentration of
power among large technology companies is cited by 9%,
with particularly limited concern in the US, home to the
leading AI developers. Only 12% of respondents say they

have no concerns about AI at all. The message is clear:
People are worried less about what AI might become than
about the harm it can already cause.
Inequality, misinformation
and privacy concerns: people’s
worries culminate in a call for
stricter regulation

Faced with these risks, the public is sending

policymakers an increasingly clear message: AI needs
stronger guardrails.  Across the sample, the net regulation
score – the share favoring strict or some regulation minus
those opposing regulation – has risen to 78%, up from
61% in 2025 (Figure 16). Support is strongest in the UK
(85%) and Spain (82%) but exceeds 70% in every surveyed
country, while only around 5% favor no regulation.
Crucially, demand for safeguards is not confined to AI
skeptics: Even respondents who expect AI to improve

their own productivity want stronger oversight. People
appear willing to embrace AI‘s benefits, but not to accept
its risks unchecked. The survey also predates the recent
wave of high-profile AI-related cybersecurity incidents,
which could further heighten public attention to safety
and accountability⁵.
⁵ See: Financial Times (2026), “Hugging Face attack is a wake-up call about the risks of AI” , 3 September 2026.

### Page 24

Allianz Research
24
Governments have taken significantly different
approaches on AI regulation, with the US and the
EU representing contrasting models (Table 1). The
US has no comprehensive federal AI law, relying on a
patchwork of state-level legislation and existing sectoral
rules, alongside federal efforts to limit divergent state
requirements. California has emerged as a leading
state regulator, introducing requirements addressing
transparency, safety and specific uses of AI. The UK

also relies largely on existing sectoral and cross-cutting
legislation, with additional measures under consideration
where gaps emerge. The EU, by contrast, has established
a comprehensive, risk-based framework through its AI
Act. In force since 2024, its obligations are being phased
in through 2028, with member states responsible for much
of the national enforcement framework. Italy has also
introduced supplementary national legislation, including

criminal provisions⁶. Outside the EU, public demand for
regulation thus runs well ahead of the rules in place.
The policy challenge is not simply to regulate
more, but to regulate what matters without stifling
innovation. Too little regulation can leave firms with
weak incentives to account for safety risks and other
harms, while allowing concentration in areas such as
hardware and data to entrench incumbents. Too much
or poorly designed regulation can raise the cost of

experimentation, slow AI adoption and investment and
weigh disproportionately on smaller firms, which are
less able to absorb compliance costs⁷. The objective for
policymakers should therefore be to address harmful
externalities and preserve trust and competition while
keeping barriers to experimentation and diffusion as
low as possible. As public demand for regulation rises,
getting this balance right will become increasingly
important.

### Page 25

14 October 2026
25
For Europe, the central challenge is not simply to accelerate AI adoption, but to turn widespread experimentation
into productive, inclusive and trusted use Curiosity is not the main constraint: Europeans have embraced AI almost as
widely as Americans, but the technology has made fewer inroads into the workplace, and workers are less prepared to
use it effectively. The priority is therefore to remove the barriers that prevent AI from translating into productivity gains

and broader economic benefits. This requires action on five fronts:
1. Move AI from private experimentation into productive workplace use.  General-purpose technologies like
AI generate productivity gains only after firms make large complementary investments. These investments
show up as costs today, dragging down earnings and productivity in the short run, the so-called productivity
J-curve. Because the costs are immediate while the benefits are delayed, private investment may fall short,

in particular for SMEs, which face disproportionately high costs for the same implementation effort⁸. Europe
should reduce these costs that are holding back implementation, especially for SMEs, through accelerated
depreciation or temporary tax credits for identifiable complementary investments such as software integration,
data infrastructure, cybersecurity and process redesign. Public support should be tied to actual deployment
in business processes and, where possible, measurable productivity or efficiency gains rather than generic AI

subscriptions.
2. Close the training gap by starting with employers. This is primarily a provision problem: many workers
receive no offer of AI training, while most of those offered it take it up or plan to do so. Employer-led training
should therefore be supported through training tax credits, public co-financing and individual learning accounts,
with a focus on occupation-specific skills and groups currently least served including women, mid-career workers,

the secondary-educated and employees in smaller firms. Europe already has useful instruments: Germany’s
“Qualifizierungschancengesetz” subsidizes training costs and, in some cases, wage costs for employees affected
by technological change, while the EU  encourages member states to develop individual learning accounts that
give workers portable training entitlements. Where firms receive public support for major AI investments, this
could be linked to a workforce-training plan.

3. Shift labor-market policy from protecting jobs to supporting transitions.  Because AI is likely to change tasks
and occupations more extensively than it eliminates jobs outright, governments need to identify adjustment
pressures before they translate into unemployment. Combining occupation-level AI-exposure indicators
with vacancy, wage and skill-demand data can help identify vulnerable workers and emerging employment
opportunities. Workers in exposed occupations should have access to retraining while still employed, stronger

job-matching services, mobility grants and temporary wage insurance where moving into a new role initially
means lower pay. Such measures must go beyond traditional active labor-market programs, whose short-term
effects are often limited and vary considerably across program types and participants. Better targeting is crucial:
Policy priorities:
adopting AI better, not just faster
⁸  For productivity discussion, see: Brynjolfsson, Rock & Syverson 2021. „The Productivity J-Curve: How Intangibles Complement General Purpose

Technologies,“ American Economic Journal: Macroeconomics. For SMEs discussion, see: OECD 2025, “ AI adoption by small and medium-sized enter -
prises”,

### Page 26

Allianz Research
26
programs that invest in human capital accumulation, such as classroom-based vocational training and on-the-
job traineeships, show large gains, in particular for the long-term unemployed, whose skills have eroded during
extended periods out of work, and for women⁹.  Recent evidence confirms that well-designed interventions
do indeed deliver: cost-benefit analyses show that such training schemes can be highly cost effective, and

programs combining part-time work placements with training and short-term community work placements for
the unemployed produce lasting earnings gains for participants¹⁰. The key lesson is therefore to replace broad,
expensive programs with measures that are tightly linked to real-time labor-market signals.
4. Make sure the productivity dividend is broadly shared. AI is likely to raise productivity but could also
shift national income from labor to capital, and if automation outruns capital accumulation, wages could fall

even while output rises¹¹. Policy should therefore address both labor income and household wealth. On the
income side, broader access to skills can help translate productivity gains into better employment prospects
and wages. . On the asset side, funded pensions, employee share plans and wider household capital-market
participation can help more people benefit directly from AI-driven returns, particularly in Europe, where  equity
ownership is heavily concentrated among wealthier households¹². At the same time, the tax system should

avoid unnecessarily favoring capital over labor and adapt if a larger share of income shifts toward capital and
economic rents. Europe is particularly exposed here, as its tax systems rely more heavily on labor income taxes
and social contributions than the US, potentially putting greater pressure on public finances if the labor share of
income declines¹³.
5. Strengthen trust by targeting the risks people actually identify. Misinformation, data misuse and labor-

and human oversight and appeal rights for high-impact AI decisions. Rules should be credible and proportionate:
safeguards that build trust can support adoption, while complex or inconsistent requirements can slow diffusion,
especially for smaller firms. EU-wide templates, sector-specific guidance and regulatory sandboxes can help
make implementation more predictable without weakening protections.

learnt and implications for other countries ” ; and evidence on the effectiveness of Ireland’s Community Employment and Tús schemes: OECD, 2024:
“Impact Evaluation of Ireland’s Active Labour Market Policies ”,

The survey queried more than 8,000 respondents across eight countries: Austria, France, Germany, Italy, Poland, Spain,
the United Kingdom and the United States. It was conducted as a web-based survey between 22 April and 25 May 2026.
Overall responsibility lies with Allianz Research, Allianz Investment Management SE. Sample planning and fieldwork

were carried out by Qualtrics.
Respondents were selected using representative quota sampling. Qualtrics was given quotas specifying how many
people to survey in each country and which selection criteria to apply, with quotas set in line with official statistics for
gender and age groups. A comparison with official statistics confirms that the resulting sample broadly corresponds to
the population aged 18 and over in each of the eight countries.

## Document 293: CTCP TCT Nước - Môi trường Bình Dương - Báo cáo cập nhật 2Q2026 (2026-09-23)

Report source: KBSV (Company)
Date: 2026-09-23
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh được 
thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng nóng 
hơn; trong khi triển vọng nhóm 
khách hàng công nghiệp bị ảnh 
hưởng bởi bất lợi thuế quan

Đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyện và xử 
lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho phép 
cải thiện doanh thu và lợi nhuận của 
mảng này

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 
785 tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An vận hành từ Q3/2025 
giúp sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài của các đợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ sở để 
chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phước trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế 
quan có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xuất khẩu truyền thống 
của Việt Nam.

UBND TP.HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng 
mạnh từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh 
trong thực tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự 
án của doanh nghiệp ở mảng này sẽ vận hành hiệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và 
lợi nhuận gộp của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ 
VND lần lượt trong 2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA với 
BWE, giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ vọng 42.2% so với 
giá đóng cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Cơ cấu doanh thu 2025 Hoạt động kinh doanh 
 Biwase là doanh nghiệp hàng đầu 
trong lĩnh vực cung cấp nước sạch với 
thị phần đạt 100% tại Bình Dương. 
Ngoài ra, công ty còn mở rộng sang 
lĩnh vực đầu tư nhà máy xử lý nước và 
chất thải rắn nhằm hoàn thiện hệ 
sinh thái dịch vụ hạ tầng môi trường 
tại khu vực. 
Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Điểm nhấn đầu tư  Chú thích 
Nắng nóng thúc đẩy tiêu thụ nước sạch khối dân cư, đem về tổng doanh thu cấp nước tại Bình Dương và 
Bình Phước năm 2026/2027 đạt 2,953/3,192 tỷ VND (6.8%/7.4%). 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo 
Nhà máy nước Nhị Thành giai đoạn 3 đi vào hoạt động giúp tổng doanh thu nhà máy đạt lần lượt 213/247 tỷ 
VND (+16%/16% YoY) trong 2026/2027 và duy trì tăng trưởng trung bình 15% đến 2030. 
 Tham khảo trong báo cáo kỳ trước: 
tại đây 
BWE hưởng lợi từ khung giá xử lý rác thải được phê duyệt, giúp doanh thu đạt 1,069/1,234 tỷ VND trong 
2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% 
 Chi tiết ở phần dưới của báo cáo

HOẠT ĐỘNG KINH DOANH 
Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số 
 Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 
325 tỷ VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ). Mảng cung cấp nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp 
tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 785 tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% 
QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An vận hành từ Q3/2025. Kinh doanh tăng trưởng 
tích cực là nhờ giá nước đầu vào mua từ TDM thấp hơn giúp tăng biên lợi nhuận 
gộp, cùng với không phát sinh lỗ tỷ giá như cùng kỳ năm ngoái.

### Page 4

4

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh 
được thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng 
nóng hơn; trong khi triển vọng 
nhóm khách hàng công nghiệp bị 
ảnh hưởng bởi bất lợi thuế quan 
 Dự báo đồng thuận xác suất xảy ra El Nino ở mức xấp xỉ 100% từ 2H2026 kéo 
dài đến tháng đầu 2027 cho thấy cường độ và thời gian kéo dài của các đợt 
nắng nóng sẽ gay gắt và kéo dài hơn so với các năm trước. Đây là cơ sở để 
chúng tôi điều chỉnh tăng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phước trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY 
(+2.6/1.1 ppts). Trái lại, sau khi section 301 được công bố và có hiệu lực thay 
thế section 122, tình hình thuế quan có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho 
các ngành xuất khẩu truyền thống của Việt Nam. Cụ thể, Section 301 không tự 
hết hạn và có nền tảng pháp lý chắc chắn (ít nhất là trong nhiệm kỳ của 
Trump); đồng thời áp dụng hạn ngạch thuế quan cho một lượng hàng dệt may 
nhất định của một số nước không có Việt Nam, khiến bối cảnh bất lợi cho các 
ngành sản xuất truyền thống. Tuy nhiên, với việc Bình Dương đang tập trung 
đầu tư hạ tầng khu công nghiệp hiện đại nhằm thu hút dòng vốn FDI vào các 
lĩnh vực công nghệ cao, sản lượng nước sạch sẽ được duy trì khi tiêu thụ khối 
sản xuất truyền thống được bù đắp bởi nhóm công nghệ cao. Theo đó, trong 
năm 2026, KBSV điều chỉnh tăng nhẹ dự báo sản lượng nước sạch tại Bình 
Dương & Bình Phước đạt 224.9 triệu m3 (+6.8% YoY), với mức tăng của nhóm 
sản xuất/sinh hoạt/hành chính lần lượt là 5.8%/7.5%/11% YoY.

Biểu đồ 4. Xác suất tăng dần của EL Nino

Nguồn: Bloomberg, KBSV tổng hợp

Mảng cấp nước: Tiến độ điều chỉnh 
giá bán nước gián đoạn do môi 
trường lạm phát leo cao 
 Do tác động của cuộc chiến Mỹ - Iran gây ra gián đoạn dòng chảy vận tải dầu 
tại eo biển Hormuz, giá nhiên liệu trong nước đã tăng mạnh từ đầu năm (Dầu 
Diesel - +64.3%, E5 RON 92 – +40%) và khiến cho tình hình lạm phát trong nước 
diễn biến tiêu cực: CPI bình quân 8 tháng năm 2026 tăng 4.5% với chỉ số giá 
nhóm nhà ở, điện nước, chất đốt và vật liệu xây dựng tăng cao nhất với 6.7% và 
giao thông tăng 5.4%. Trong bối cảnh đó, chúng tôi cho rằng tiến độ điều 
chỉnh giá bán nước tại các thị trường của BWE có khả năng bị đẩy lùi sang 
2027-2028 hoặc cho đến khi tình hình giá nhiên liệu ổn định trở lại. Trong dài 
hạn, khả năng đàm phán giá nước thành công là khá cao bởi: i) Thông tư 
145/2025/TT-BTC có hiệu lực từ 2026 là căn cứ để UBND quy định tỷ suất lợi 
nhuận định mức các dự án nhà máy nước; ii) BWE và cổ đông lớn là TDM dự 
kiến đầu tư và mở rộng công suất cung cấp – phân phối nước tại các vùng hẻo 
lánh nên có khả năng tăng áp lực đàm phán tăng giá nước tại Bình Dương. 
Trong kịch bản thận trọng, KBSV điều chỉnh tăng giá nước sạch trung bình khu

### Page 5

5

vực Bình Dương 1%/năm từ 2028 – 2030.

Đơn giá xử thu gom, vận chuyện và 
xử lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho 
phép cải thiện doanh thu và lợi 
nhuận của mảng này 
 UBND TP.HCM (bao gồm TP.HCM cũ, Bình Dương, Bà Rịa Vũng Tàu) quy định 
giá cụ thể đối với dịch vụ vận chuyển, xử lý chất thải rắn sinh hoạt trên địa bàn. 
Theo đó, quy định mới thay thế văn bản có hiệu lực từ 2016 sẽ chuyển hình 
thức tính giá đầy đủ theo 3 cấu phần: Thu gom, vận chuyển và Xử lý; với khung 
giá được xác định cho các phường đô thị và xã/nông thôn. So với quy định 
2016, quy định mới điều chỉnh tăng mạnh khung giá khoán đối với chủ nguồn 
thải nhỏ (Hộ gia đình tăng 80-184% đối với khu vực đô thị, 55-140% đối với khu 
vực nông thôn; Hộ kinh doanh nhỏ lẻ tăng 42-150% đối với khu vực đô thị, 20-
138% đối với khu vực nông thôn); đồng thời yêu cầu chủ thải tại nguồn và chủ 
nguồn thải lớn áp dụng khung giá thành (bao gồm giá thu gom, vận chuyển và 
xử lý) nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn chi phí liên quan tới các khâu xử 
lý rác thải thực tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng việc ban hành khung giá mới tính toán 
đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh phản ánh định hướng phát triển 
hạ tầng xử lý rác thải hiện đại của TP.HCM, đặt trọng tâm vào các nhà máy đốt 
rác phát điện thân thiện và an toàn với môi trường. Đối với doanh nghiệp hoạt 
động trong lĩnh vực môi trường nhiều kinh nghiệm như BWE, cơ chế thu gom, 
vận chuyển và xử lý minh bạch sẽ giúp thúc đẩy hiệu quả các dự án mà doanh 
nghiệp có kế hoạch triển khai trong tương lai. KBSV dự báo doanh thu và lợi 
nhuận gộp của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ 
VND lần lượt trong 2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Nguồn: UBND TP .HCM, KBSV

Biwase thành công tăng vốn cổ 
phần lên 2,513 tỷ VND 
 Sau đợt chào báo cho cổ đông hiện hữu với giá chào bán 37,000 đồng/cổ 
phiếu, doanh nghiệp dự kiến huy động được khoảng 1,162.5 tỷ VND, nâng vốn 
điều lệ lên mức 2,513 tỷ VND. Khoản vốn bổ sung được dự kiến sử dụng cho ba 
mục đích chính: thanh toán nợ vay đến hạn (khoảng 773.3 tỷ đồng), đầu tư dự 
án nhà máy điện rác công suất 24MW (225 tỷ đồng) và mua cổ phần tại CTCP 
Cấp nước Ninh Thuận (hơn 164 tỷ đồng), qua đó biến doanh nghiệp này trở 
thành công ty liên kết. Dự kiến, lợi nhuận đóng góp từ công ty liên kết trong 
2026/2027 tiếp tục tăng trưởng tích cực, đạt 238.1/245.2 tỷ VND (40%/3% YoY).

Nguồn: Báo cáo công ty, KBSV

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Về diễn biến thị trường, BWE hiện đang giao dịch quanh mức -1 độ lệch chuẩn 
trung bình 5 năm EV/EBITDA, phản ánh những bất ổn trong thương mại toàn 
cầu ảnh hưởng tới tiêu thụ nước phục vụ sản xuất. Tuy nhiên, chúng tôi đánh 
giá triển vọng trong tương lai của mảng môi trường (bao gồm nước thải và rác 
thải) cộng với chiến lược đa dạng hóa địa bàn cung cấp nước sẽ bù đắp được 
KQKD tăng trưởng chậm lại.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA 
với BWE, giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND/cổ phiếu, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ 
vọng 42.2% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 22/09/2026. Mức EV/EBITDA mục tiêu cho 
việc định giá doanh nghiệp là 8.8 lần, tương ứng với mức trung bình 5 năm của 
doanh nghiệp, để phản ánh sự cải thiện KQKD đến từ mảng môi trường và cơ 
chế giảm giá mua nước từ TDM sẽ bù đắp sự giảm tốc của mảng cấp nước trong 
ngắn hạn. Với mức giá mục tiêu trên, cổ phiếu được định giá tương đương P/B 
1.7x cho 2026, phù hợp với việc ROE được kỳ vọng cải thiện lên mức 14.1%.

## Document 307: Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Energy shock: Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022
10/2/2026 2
Will the same causes produce the same effects? In other words, will the war in Iran and the resulting
surge in oil and gas prices lead to an inflationary shock comparable to that seen in 2022? Will their
negative effects on growth be the same as those of the war in Ukraine and the subsequent energy
shock?
We have selected a set of indicators to track the impact of this new energy shock — caused by the war

in the Middle East — on activity and prices in the Eurozone, the United States, oil and gas markets and
emerging countries, and to see how much the current situation resembles that of 2022 at the outbreak
of the conflict in Ukraine.
This dashboard features charts and comments that will be updated on a monthly basis for as long as
necessary.
Overall, based on data available through September 2026, economic activity continues to show
resilience. The high levels of business climate surveys illustrate this well. Household confidence,

however, is more vulnerable and sensitive to energy price pressures. Financing conditions in emerging
economies also continue to hold up well against the shock (much better than in 2022) as well as
against the tensions in DM bond markets, which intensified this summer, partly in the wake of the
reinforcing upward pressure on energy prices. Higher energy prices are also fueling inflationary
pressures and pushing inflation even higher. In particular, Eurozone inflation rose by 0.6 percentage

points to 3.8% year-on-year (September flash estimate), reaching its highest level in three years.

## Document 371: Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-10-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain (2026-10-20).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#china #asia #aud
Q3 China GDP above expectations, but downside risks remain
By Elliot Clarke
Head of International Economics, Westpac
16:03 October 19 2026 Share
Stimulus is required if success in 2026 is to be sustained through 2027 and 2028.
China GDP surprised to the upside in Q3 2026, quarterly growth accelerating from 0.9% in Q2 to 1.2%
in Q3. Annual growth lifted from 4.3% to 4.5% as a result; though, on a year-to-date basis, growth
edged down 0.1ppts to 4.6%. The Q3 result keeps authorities on track to achieve their 4.5-5.0%ytd

ambition for 2026, and also provides a little more time to accelerate domestic demand into 2027.

### Page 2

Broad-based stimulus is required because net exports’ contribution to growth will likely abate in coming
quarters now that the trade surplus is back at record highs, and as many Chinese firms are
increasingly splitting their focus between expanding domestic capacity and offshore production
networks.

While annual retail sales growth accelerated from 0.4% to 1.1% in September, the Q3 year-to-date
growth pulse of 1.1% is just a quarter of the gain reported a year ago. Automobiles, construction
materials and jewellery have been particular heavy detractors over the past year, but most of the other
sub-categories also lack momentum. As we detailed in our September Market Outlook, this is not
because households are cash constrained, but rather as sentiment is extremely weak and the outlook
for employment and wealth highly uncertain. Key for wealth is that house prices for new and
established homes continue to decline month-on-month (respectively 0.1% and 0.3% in September)
five years into the downturn. Property investment’s ongoing decline (-20.6%ytd) suggests this
persistent weakness in sales and prices is demand driven.

While total fixed asset investment has been an outright negative for annual GDP growth since April, a
recovery is not beyond either the public or private sector’s capacity. Rather, across the economy,
entities are waiting for direction and incentive from the Central Government. This is likely to come by
early-2027 to offset the loss of support from trade, and as downside risks to global growth become
more apparent. Helpfully, industrial production continues to grow at a robust pace, 5.3%ytd, borrowing
rates for businesses and households are low, and authorities are beginning to provide banks with
additional lending capacity. For larger businesses, anecdotes suggest officials are also encouraging
major institutional investors to increase their structural allocation to domestic equities, building lasting 
appetite for new equity issuance, which in turn will fund future waves of real economic capacity and
income generation.

We remain comfortable with the view that Chinese GDP growth will hold at or slightly above 4.5%
through 2026–2028. But, the longer the Central Government waits to trigger new real investment
across both the private and public sectors, the higher the likelihood of poor sentiment becoming
entrenched and downside risks crystallising. If authorities wait until after net exports’ contribution has
faded, even holding annual aggregate growth above 4.0% could become a significant challenge. For
this reason, we expect material stimulus initiatives to be announced in the next 3 to 6 months to reset
the property sector and turn the business investment trend.

## Document 387: Strategic product vulnerability_ China least, then Europe and then the US (2026-09-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Strategic product vulnerability_ China least, then Europe and then the US (2026-09-16).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-16
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

16 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
SOURCE: INSTITUTE OF INTERNATIONAL FINANCE, LSEG, MACROBONDCHART
STRATEGIC PRODUCT VULNERABILITY? CHINA LEAST, THEN EUROPE AND THEN THE US
Koen De Leus
“Germany is too dependent on the US for its security, on Russia for its energy and on China for its exports.” That was, in essence, Brookings’
Constanze Stelzenmüller’s diagnosis in June 2022, and it was – and is – valid as well for Europe as a whole.

But does this dependency also apply to technological and industrial products? The European Commission’s EXternal Vulnerability Index
(EXVI) answers that very question, mapping out the EU’s exposure to foreign supply chains.
Relatively good results for the EU
The EU is relatively well positioned, with a score of 0.18 on net-zero technologies and 0.22 on semiconductors. Raw materials appear to be the
weakest point (0.28). Interestingly, compared to its peers, the EU is more vulnerable than China across all three chains (0.13), but significantly

less vulnerable than the US overall (0.22 vs. 0.28). The only sector where the EU score is weaker than the US one is on semi-conductors, and
the difference is not large (0.22 vs 0.19). Like the EU, the US’ vulnerability is biggest in raw material (0.32). This is, among other factors, due to
outsourcing polluting mining starting in the 1990s. Looking at the essential components for the green transition, the EU score on raw materials is
concerning, but the better one on net-zero technologies is more encouraging.

The EU challenge is not its dependencies but its relatively weak global position
Taking a closer look at the two pillars across the different products, it becomes clear that Europe’s problem is mainly related to its global position
(Pillar 2). Dependency (Pillar 1) itself is not the core issue, with much lower scores than the ones for Pillar 2. The EU dependency risk on raw mate-
rials is even comparable to China score (0.24 vs 0.23), and the EU score on semi-conductors (0.13) is the lowest of the other results for the region.

On the other hand, Pillar 2 scores are more negative, illustrating the lack of EU competitiveness in these areas, with scores hovering between 0.29
for net-zero technologies up to a high 0.39 for semiconductors and 0.41 for raw materials. Europe simply does not produce enough of these pro -
ducts at competitive prices, as big European companies in these sectors are limited. Take semiconductors, for example. Imports are not extremely
EXVI is built on two pillars, with two simple questions for each pr oduct: 1/ How dependent is a r egion on a handful of for eign suppliers? 2/ How

globally competitive is it in that pr oduct measured by its imports versus e xports? The result is a single score from 0 (low vulnerability) to 1
(high). A region scores high (vulnerable) when ther e is both a heavy  reliance on others and a weak market position.
Strategic product vulnerability? China least, then Europe and then the US

### Page 2

2
La banque
d’un monde
qui change
Chart of the Week / 16 September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
One trend is undeniable: China consistently enjoys the lowest vulnerability. Decades of strategic plans, as well as subsidies and other practices
to restrict open markets, have enabled it to build up domestic supplies and stocks.
The EU’s multiple responses
Looking ahead, the EU can deploy a number of options in response, from circularity to domestic mining, including international partnerships.

Brussels is aiming to mine or recycle 10% of critical materials by 2030, but there is paradox at play here, as circularity only works once sufficient
volumes have first been mined and used. Therefore, in the coming years, international partnerships will be a priority and, for that matter, the EU
has been very active on that front since the start of the second Trump presidency.
The EU is aware of the challenges that it facesAnd the EXVI indices are particularly relevant for assessing supply chains targeted by major policy

initiatives, such as the Chips Act (strengthening the EU’s semiconductor ecosystem and technological sovereignty), the Net Zero Industry Act
(NZIA) (building domestic manufacturing capacity for clean-tech products such as solar, wind and batteries) and the Critical Raw Materials Act
(CRMA) (securing and diversifying the EU’s supply of critical and strategic raw materials). The challenges have been identified and an action plan
has been drawn up. Now it needs to be implemented on the ground.

concentrated, as shown by the r elatively low Pillar 1 scor e of 0.13. However, the high Pillar 2 scor e of 0.39 shows that the EU is weak at
producing semiconductors itself. That is precisely why the European Chips Act is focusing on building domestic pr oduction capacity rather than

on diversifying imports. The same reasoning applies to raw materials and, to a lesser extent, to net-zero technologies.

### Page 2

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
No surprise in the October CPI
The new complete monthly CPI gained 3.8% in the year to 
October, coming in a touch softer than Westpac’s estimate of 
3.9%yr but a bit stronger than the market estimate of 3.6%yr. 
So while the market and other commentators may have been 
surprised by the result it was broadly within our expectations 
and as such has had just a marginal impact on our inflation 
profile.
The October CPI was flat in the month, a touch softer than 
Westpac’s published near-cast of 0.1% on the back of a larger 
than expected decline in electricity (–10.2%) which was only 
partially offset by a smaller than expected decline in auto fuel 
(–0.9%) and an unexpected bump up in garments (+2.6%).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) lifted 0.3% in the month of October, 
the same monthly increase it has seen for the previous two 
months and down from the 0.5%mth increase in July but 
stronger than the 0.2%mth prints from March to June.
This saw the annual pace of the TM increase to 3.3% from 
3.2% in September. This month the TM excluded the annual 
rise in electricity; alongside other large price rises and falls. 
Due to the short history of many series in the monthly CPI, the 
annual pace of monthly TM inflation can only be calculated 
back to April 2025. Before then the ABS notes that the annual 
pace is calculated from the quarterly CPI data.
For more information on the October monthly CPI see 
“Electrical noise is far from static ”.
Monthly CPI points to some momentum
Following the October CPI we have seen some minor revisions 
to the various components in our December quarter CPI 
estimate with the upward revisions, such as transport via auto 
fuel and motor vehicles, being offset by downward revisions 
to recreation, alcohol & tobacco and utilities in particular. This 
has left our December CPI estimate unchanged at 0.6%qtr.   
As our Chief Economist, Luci Ellis, has noted that inflation 
did see a bump in the September quarter and again in the 
month of October. However, the main sources of the surprise 
have had little to do with domestic demand or labour market 
pressures. Rather, a sizeable part of the bump looks to have 
been from administered prices and noise. Since the release of 
the data, the RBA has recognised this issue but at the post 
RBA Board Decision Media Conference in December, the 
Governor communicated that the Bank is more worried about 
upside risks to inflation than first thought.
We agree that some of this momentum will continue into the 
December quarter as highlighted by our upward revision in our 
We will continue to focus on the quarterly prints
Justin Smirk
Senior Economist

BULLETIN
December quarter TM estimate to 0.8%qtr/3.2%yr from our 
earlier estimate of 0.7%qtr/3.1%yr. 
For the December quarter, price changes that are included in 
the TM go from a low of –0.26% to a high of 1.66%. Readers 
will remember that the TM is calculated from seasonally 
adjusted price changes and not the original estimates so if 
unaffected by regular seasonal price shifts. We estimate 
that the most significant components to be trimmed off 
the top in the quarter are: electricity (2.7%); other services 
for motor vehicles (2.5%); tobacco (2.1%); and automotive 
fuel (1.7%). Trimmed off the bottom are: other non-durable 
household products (–0.39%); garments for women (–0.92%); 
international holiday travel (–0.97%); vegetables (–1.16%) and 
wine (–1.27%).   
However, we do not expect this momentum to continue 
through 2026. As Luci noted in her article “ RBA on hold 
in 2026, risks on both sides ” we expect inflation to keep 
easing, due to the lagged impact of past restrictive policy 
and the unwind of earlier government support measures. We 
believe that many commentators have overlooked these lags. 
Monetary policy typically takes a year or more to fully affect 
jobs and prices, so current outcomes reflect the peak cash rate 
from a year ago and we expect to see further labour market 
softening. Strong hiring across the care economy has masked 
some of that restrictiveness, as has public infrastructure 
spending, but these supports are now fading along with last 
year’s tax-cut boost. We believe that even with the 75bps in  
rate cuts this year, policy remains mildly restrictive, and given 
usual lags, this will keep showing in the data through 2026.
Taking a look at some of the recent larger moves in monthly 
prices can highlight many of the issues Luci raises. Taking the 
seasonally adjusted data, smoothing it with a three month 
average then calculating the change over three months (so we 
have three months on three months percentage change) we 
find at the top end of the list some price increases you would 
not expect to continue at the same pace. 
For example at the top are: lamb & goat (8.1%, highly 
seasonal); accessories (8.1% with jewellery prices surging 
on the back of record high gold prices); audio visual & 
computing services (6.0% due to Netflix subscriptions); beef 
& veal (3.7%, highly seasonal); coffee & tea (3.6% noting 
global coffee prices have passed their peak and are already 
significantly lower); water & sewerage (2.3% IPART decision 
was for a 13.8% increase for Sydney Water in October); 
property rates & charges (2.3% administered prices); and 
electricity (1.2%, reflecting unwinding of cost of living 
rebates). Offsetting some of these increases was falling prices 
for: men’s footwear (–2.0%); vegetables (–2.0% seasonal); 
eggs (–1.5%, reflecting the unwinding of the price spike 
associated with an outbreak of bird flu); and major household 
appliances (–1.2%). 
Core inflation still to moderate through 2026
We are projecting headline inflation to get back to the RBA’s 
target band, but not until later in 2026. We now see core 
inflation back below the midpoint of the 2–3% range at the 
end of the year. We had thought this might be achieved by the 
third quarter but now expect current momentum to continue

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
for a bit longer. However, it is worth noting that our estimate
of the six month annualised pace of the TM is down to 2.4%yr
by June 2026.
Electricity to boost November CPI
Westpac is estimating a 0.4% increase in the November

monthly CPI which will hold the annual pace flat at 3.8%yr.
The seasonally adjusted estimate is 0.5% in the month
suggesting it is a stronger than usual estimate.
Boosting the November estimate is: a 16% surge in electricity
(as the impact of the last round of rebates fades); a 3.2% jump
in gas & other household fuels; and a 2.3% bump in auto fuel
(based on reported pump prices). Partially offsetting these
gains is a –2.6% decline in holiday travel & accommodation

and a –2.9% drop in clothing & footwear.
Take care with the Monthly CPI
Many series in the monthly CPI only date back to early 2024
so seasonal adjustment is somewhat problematic. Some
series did have a longer monthly history coming from the
previously published monthly CPI indicator and it appears
the ABS may have used some of this to seasonally adjust the
data. However, we caution that some of the new data has a
different history to the old data and as such, we expect it is

going to take some time to understand the seasonal behaviour
of the new data.
For example, for male garments there is some difference
with the reported monthly change compared to the old CPI
Indicator series but this appears to be only marginal. However,
for women’s, the difference can be significant with even a
reversal of the sign of change in some recent months. And for
some series with a short history and no monthly indicator data
to compare to some of the adjustment appears to be a little

arbitrary, as you can see in the chart right which compares the
children’s garments index in original and seasonally adjusted
terms. The only adjustment made to the series was in June
2025 when a –3.2% change was adjusted to a flat print. Why?
We are yet to uncover.
You can also see where the ABS has turned to smoothing
processes to seasonally adjust short data series. In the chart
over the seasonally adjusted series looks as if has been
generated by applying a Henderson Trend to the original

children’s footwear series.
As such, we expect it is possible there will be significant
adjustments made to the seasonally adjustment process as
data is collected which could result in significant revisions to
seasonally adjusted series including the Trimmed Mean and
the Weighted Median.

荷姆茲海峽運輸仍充滿變數 ，布油價格將維持在 90

《天然氣價格：美持平或略低於去年 ，歐洲偏上行 》
美國鑽井活動緩升 ，增加較多在天然氣 ，隨美國新
的液化天然氣出口工廠上線 ，逐漸改善管道瓶頸 ，
天然氣 10月庫存僅高於五年均值 2%，注入期可持
續增至11月中，預估今年冬天暖氣度日數將持平去
年 ， 並 比 過 去 五 個 冬 季 的 均 值 低1% ， 預估美國
4Q26天然氣價格將因季節性走升，但持平或略低於
去年。卡達能源LNG船已出口 ，但非往歐洲 ，歐洲
儲氣量雖拉升 ，但僅達 70%，遠低於過往 ，TTF價
格須優於亞洲以吸引運往亞洲的 LNG轉往歐洲 ，加

上中東地緣緊張，預期4Q26 TTF價格仍有上行風險。

長，維持股東回報 ，較看好
綜合油企。

## Document 492: オンチェーン時代の勝者は誰か ～銀行・証券会社の外側に広がる新たな勝機～

Source PDF: all report pdf/オンチェーン時代の勝者は誰か ～銀行・証券会社の外側に広がる新たな勝機～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年10 月 20 日

1/5 
オンチェーン時代の勝者は誰か 
～銀行・証券会社の外側に広がる新たな勝機～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  柏村 祐

（要旨） 
○ 個人投資家にとって当たり前であった「銀行や証券会社を通じた取引」という常識が、ブロ
ックチェーン技術を基盤とするオンチェーン市場の台頭により覆ろうとしている。 
○ 為替、決済、証券の各分野において、既存インフラの外側で新たな市場が拡大している。ス
テーブルコインを通じた新たなドル取引市場の形成や、トークン化株式による少額投資機会
の拡大がみられる。また、決済分野では、参加者の割合や資金補充の頻度を想定した試算に
より、新規システムでも、条件が整えば既存システムより低コストで決済できる可能性が示
されている。 
○ オンチェーン市場の拡大により、既存の金融機関の収益構造や競争環境が変化する可能性が
ある。個人投資家は、伝統的金融株の位置づけを再点検するとともに、トークン化資産やス
テーブルコインといった新たな投資機会について、リスクを十分に理解した上で、ポートフ
ォリオへの組み入れを検討する準備を始めるべきである。

1. 既存の金融機関は、これからも「主役」であり続けるのか？  
個人投資家にとって、資産運用といえば「銀行や証券会社に口座を開き、そこを通じて株式や
投資信託、外貨を売買する」のが当たり前である。私たちが金融市場にアクセスするためには、
こうした「窓口」を通るのが一般的であり、伝統的な金融機関が提供するインフラと手数料体系
を受け入れるのが当然とされてきた。しかし、ブロックチェーン技術を基盤とする「オンチェー
ン市場」の台頭により、この前提が変わりつつある。 
「暗号資産（仮想通貨） 」と聞くと、多くの個人投資家は価格変動の激しい投機対象を思い浮か
べるかもしれない。だが、現在起きている変化の本質はそこではない。米ドルなどの法定通貨に
価値を連動させるステーブルコインや、株式・債券などの現実資産（RWA：Real World Assets）
をデジタル化したトークン化資産（RWAトークン）の普及により、従来の銀行や証券会社とは異な
る事業者やネットワークを通じた「もう一つの金融インフラ」が、実用化に向けて急速に発展し
ている。 
既存の金融機関を通さずに、低コストかつ24 時間365日、グローバルな金融商品にアクセスで
きるようになれば、伝統的な金融インフラの位置づけは揺らぐ。オンチェーン市場はすでにニッ

### Page 2

2/5 
チな実験の段階を脱しつつあり、個人投資家にとっても無視できない存在になっている。 
本稿では、為替、決済、証券という金融の3 つのコア領域において、オンチェーン市場が既存
システムの外側でどのように拡大しているのかを最新のデータをもとに整理し、個人投資家が取
るべき次の一手を提示したい。

2. 銀行・証券会社の外側で拡大するオンチェーン市場 
(1) 既存の為替市場の外側に、新しいドル取引市場が生まれている 
ステーブルコインの普及により、既存の為替市場とは異なる経路を通じた新たな資金フローが
形成されている。BIS（国際決済銀行）が2026年 3月に公表した論文「Stablecoin flows and 
spillovers to FX markets」によれば、法定通貨から米ドルペッグのステーブルコインへの累積
純流入額は急増しており、資料 1に示された直近時点では約950億ドルに達している。 
ここで注目すべきは、流入元の通貨内訳である。同論文によれば、法定通貨からステーブルコ
インへの交換の7 割超は、米ドル以外の通貨によるものである。これは、ステーブルコイン市場
が単なる暗号資産の取引手段にとどまらず、既存の為替市場（FX市場）の外側に、実質的な「新
しいドル取引市場」を形成していることを意味する。特にマクロ経済が不安定な国や資本規制の
ある国ほど、ステーブルコイン経由でドルを得るコストが割高になっており、現地のドル需要の
強さがうかがえる。同論文は、こうした資金流入が現地通貨安を招くなど、既存の為替市場にも
影響を及ぼしていることを実証している。

資料 1 法定通貨からステーブルコインへの累積純流入 
（注）法定通貨から米ドルペッグのステーブルコイン（USDT、USDC、DAI、BUSD）への日次純流入の累積額（10 億米ドル）。 
（出所）BIS, “Stablecoin flows and spillovers to FX markets”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(2) 新規システムも、一定の参加者を獲得すれば既存システムより低コストになり得る

### Page 3

3/5 
こうしたオンチェーン上の新規システムは、既存の銀行・証券インフラを代替し得るのだろう
か。スタンフォード大学のダレル・ダフィー教授が2026年 8 月のカンザスシティ連邦準備銀行主
催のシンポジウム（ジャクソンホール会議）で発表した論文では、トークン化された新たな決済
システム（以下、新規システム）と既存システムのコスト比較が行われている。 
金融インフラはネットワーク効果が強く働くため、初期段階では参加者が少なく、流動性の分
散によってコストが割高になりがちである。しかし、同論文のモデル試算によれば、新規システ
ムが一定の参加者を獲得し、より短いサイクルでの資金補充（例えば1日 10回、T′=0.10）が可
能になれば、状況は逆転する。資料2 の通り、新規システムの参加率が16％の場合、決済額100
万ドルあたりの清算コストは既存システム（レガシー型）の約3.5ドルに対し、新規型は約2.5
ドルとなる。この結果は、新規システムであっても、一定の参加者を獲得し、資金補充の頻度な
どの条件が整えば、既存システムよりも低コストで運営できる可能性を示している。

資料 2 既存システムと新規システムの清算コスト比較 
（注）100 万ドルの決済あたりの清算コスト（損益分岐水準、ドル） 。青色はレガシー型（従来システム）、赤色は新規型（ 資金補充
間隔 T′=0.10 日）、緑色は新規型（資金補充間隔 T′=0.50 日）を示す。横軸は新規システム参加者の割合。数値はモデルに
よる試算。 
（出所）Darrell Duffie, “Tokenized Finance and The Perimeter of Central Banking”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(3) 少額投資家にも、オンチェーンを通じた新たな株式投資の機会が広がっている 
オンチェーン化の波は、決済や為替だけでなく、証券投資の領域にも及んでいる。IMF（国際通
貨基金）が2026年 10月に公表した「国際金融安定性報告書（GFSR）」では、トークン化された株
式市場の動向が分析されている。 
トークン化株式の大きな特徴は、24時間365日の取引が可能であることに加え、株式の「小口
化（Fractionalization） 」が容易な点にある。資料3 は、トークン化株式の取引サイズの内訳を
示しているが、中央集権型取引所（CEX）および分散型取引所（DEX）のいずれの市場において
も、株式市場の通常取引時間内外を問わず取引の約 80％が「1株未満」の単位で行われている。
これは、オンチェーン市場において株式の小口化に対する需要が強いことを示しており、少額で
投資する個人投資家の参加がうかがえる。従来の証券会社でも少額投資は可能だが、オンチェー
ン技術の普及によって投資家が利用できる取引経路が多様化し、既存の証券市場の外側にも新た

### Page 4

4/5 
な投資機会が広がりつつある。

資料 3 トークン化株式の取引サイズ（1 株未満 vs 1 株以上） 
（注）IMF が分析対象とした主要 5 種類のトークン化株式等における、1 株未満と 1 株以上の取引件数の割合。CEX は中央集権
型取引所、DEX は分散型取引所を示す。取引時間内（マーケットアワー）と取引時間外（マーケットアワー外）で比較。 
（出所）IMF, “Global Financial Stability Report, October 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

3. オンチェーン時代の勝者となるための投資戦略 
第 2 章で見てきたように、為替、決済、証券の各分野において、既存の金融機関の「外側」
に、より効率的でアクセスしやすい新たな市場が育ちつつある。初期段階では流動性やコスト面
で課題を抱えるオンチェーンシステムも、参加者が一定規模を超えれば既存インフラより低コス
トになり得る。では、冒頭の問いに戻ろう。既存の金融機関はこれからも主役であり続けるの
か。銀行や証券会社が金融市場の主要な担い手であり続けるとしても、オンチェーン市場の拡大
によって、手数料収入に依存する従来のビジネスモデルは変革を迫られると筆者はみる。この
「金融インフラの競争構造の変化」を見据え、個人投資家は以下の3つの視点で自身の投資戦略
を見直す必要がある。 
第一に、ポートフォリオにおける「伝統的金融株」の位置づけの再点検である。これまで、メ
ガバンクや大手証券会社、取引所などの金融インフラ企業は、安定した収益基盤を持つ高配当銘
柄として個人投資家に選好されてきた。しかし、オンチェーン市場の拡大は、これらの企業の収
益源である為替手数料、決済手数料、委託手数料を脅かし得る。もちろん、既存の金融機関もト
ークン化技術の取り込み（プライベートチェーンの構築など）を急いでいるが、オープンなオン
チェーン市場との競争により、長期的には手数料の低下圧力が強まる可能性が高い。金融株へ投
資する際は、単なる利回りだけでなく、 「次世代インフラへの適応力」を厳しく見極める必要があ
る。 
第二に、オンチェーンがもたらす「新たな投資機会」へのアクセス準備である。トークン化株

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5 
式のデータが示す通り、オンチェーン市場では「1株未満」の少額取引が広がり、個人投資家の新
たな投資経路となりつつある。さらに、株式以外の資産についてもトークン化の取り組みが進ん
でおり、将来的には不動産やプライベートエクイティなどへの投資機会が広がる可能性がある。
現在はまだ海外プラットフォームが中心だが、国内でも法整備が進み、トークン化資産（セキュ
リティ・トークンなど）の取り扱いが拡大している。個人投資家は、暗号資産を投機対象として
一括りにせず、ステーブルコインやRWAトークンといった新しい金融手段の特性とリスクを理解
し、自身のポートフォリオを補完する選択肢として活用できるか検討することが重要である。 
第三に、米ドルへのアクセス手段としての「米ドル連動型ステーブルコイン」の活用可能性で
ある。法定通貨からステーブルコインへの資金流入が急増している事実は、ステーブルコインが
米ドルへのアクセス手段として存在感を高めていることを示している。日本においても、将来的
な円安リスクに備える手段として、従来の銀行の外貨預金や証券会社の外貨建てMMFに加え、米
ドルペッグのステーブルコインが選択肢となる可能性がある。ただし、ステーブルコインには発
行体の信用リスクや価格の連動が失われるリスク、償還・流動性に関するリスクがあり、外貨預
金や MMFとも安全性や法的な権利が異なる点には注意が必要である。 
オンチェーン時代に求められるのは、特定の暗号資産の値上がりを当てることではなく、金融
インフラの構造変化を捉え、新たな投資手段のリスクを理解した上で、低コストかつ効率的に活
用することである。銀行・証券会社の外側で起きている変化に、今から目を向けておきたい。

以 上

【参考文献】 
・BIS (2026) “Stablecoin flows and spillovers to FX markets” 
・Darrell Duffie (2026) “Tokenized Finance and The Perimeter of Central Banking” 
・IMF (2026) “Global Financial Stability Report, October 2026”

## Document 505: ニュージーランド総選挙は新興政党がカギを握る、NZドル相場は？ ～政権交代観測とRBNZのマンデート変更がNZドルの重しに～

Source PDF: all report pdf/ニュージーランド総選挙は新興政党がカギを握る、NZドル相場は？ ～政権交代観測とRBNZのマンデート変更がNZドルの重しに～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年 10 月 1 日

1/2 
ニュージーランド総選挙は新興政党がカギを握る、NZ ドル相場は？ 
～政権交代観測と RBNZ のマンデート変更が NZ ドルの重しに～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ニュージーランドでは 10 月１日に議会が解散し、11 月７日の総選挙に向けた選挙戦が本格
化した。 二大政党の支持率低下が続くなか、 土地税の導入と年間1.94 万ＮＺドルのベーシッ
クインカムに相当する 「市民所得」 の支給を掲げる新興のオポチュニティ党が支持を伸ばし、
初の議席獲得と連立政権の行方を左右する可能性が浮上している。 世論調査では、 最大野党 ・
労働党を中心とする左派陣営が同党の支持を得れば政権を樹立し得る一方、現与党連合が過
半数を確保できるかも不透明である。 
○ 金融市場では、政権交代時にRBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）の政策目標へ「持続可能な
雇用の最大化」 が再び加えられる可能性が意識され、 ＮＺドルは対米ドルで10 ヵ月ぶりの安
値圏に弱含んでいる。足元の失業率が10 年ぶりの高水準となるなか、 マンデート変更が実現
すれば追加利上げのハードルが高まるとの見方もあり、総選挙後の連立協議と金融政策への
影響がＮＺドル相場の先行きを左右する可能性がある。

ニュージーランド議会（代議院：一院制（総議席数120） ）が10 月１日に解散し、11 月７日に実
施予定の総選挙に向けた動きが正式に始まった。 同国では、30 年前にドイツ型の小選挙区比例代表
併用制（ＭＭＰ）が導入されて以来、右派の国民党、ないし左派の労働党を中心とする連立政権が
一般的となっている。さらに、このところは二大政党の支持率の低下傾向に歯止めがかからず、多
数政党との連立による政権樹立を余儀なくされる展開が続いている。 
こうしたなか、今回の総選挙では、新興政党・オポチュニティ党が「台風の目」となる可能性が
高まっている。同党は結党から 10 年を迎えるものの、過去の総選挙では議席獲得に必要な得票率
基準（５％）を満たすことができなかった。しかし、最新の世論調査では、支持率が基準を上回っ
て初めて議席を獲得し、キャスティングボートを握る可能性が高まっている。背景には、同党が掲
げる公約が影響している。同党は、都市部で年間1.75％、農村部で0.5％の土地税を新設し、それ
を財源の一部として、 原則18 歳以上の国民などに年間 1.94 万ＮＺドルのベーシックインカム （Ｂ
Ｉ）に相当する「市民所得」を支給する税制改正を公約に掲げる。同党はその目的について、国民
生活の下支えに加え、 高止まりする住宅価格の抑制につながるとの見方を示す（図１） 。さらに、 同

### Page 2

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
党は、 土地税導入に伴い住宅価格は下落
するものの、 ＢＩの支給に伴い国民の９
割以上が現状と同等、 ないし上回る生活
水準を維持可能との独自のシミュレー
ションを公表している。なお、国民党、
労働党のいずれの党首も税制改正案の
採用を否定するなど、 実現のハードルは
高い。一方、 同党の政策には左派陣営と
親和性のあるものもみられることから、
選挙後の連立協議の行方が注目される。

最新の世論調査によれば、最大野党・労働党を中心とする左派陣営が、オポチュニティ党の支持
を得れば政権を樹立し得る情勢が示されている。一方、国民党、ＡＣＴ党、ニュージーランド・フ
ァースト党で構成される現与党連合につ
いても、単独で過半数を確保できるかは
不透明となっているこのところの金融市
場においては、通貨ＮＺドルの対米ドル
相場が 10 ヵ月ぶりの安値水準で推移す
るなど弱含んでいる（図２） 。背景には、
RBNZ（ニュージーランド準備銀行）の政
策運営が、政治的な争点となっているこ
とがある。 労働党などはRBNZ のマンデー
ト（政策目標）の変更を公約に掲げてお
り、政権交代となれば、持続可能な雇用
の最大化が再び加えられると見込まれる。足元の雇用環境を巡っては、直近４-６月の失業率が 10
年ぶりの高水準となったことが確認されている。RBNZ は９月の定例会合で追加利上げを決定した
ものの （注１）、仮に政権交代によってマンデートが変更されれば、 先行きの政策運営を巡って追加
利上げのハードルが高まるとの見方につながる可能性がある。こうした事情がＮＺドル相場の重し
になっているとみられる。足元のオセアニア通貨は、豪ドル相場も下値を探る動きをみせ るなか、
ＮＺドルを巡る状況にも注意が必要となっている。

前次投資建議：2026.06.26 
「買進」 ，目標價: $301

報告日期：2026.10.12 研 究 員：李彝安

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 26Q2 測試需求續強，湖口二廠產能爬坡速度與毛利率優於本研究部原預估，中興三廠預計
27Q1 加入。據此上修 26/27F EPS 至 9.9/13.6 元；目標價由 301 元上調至 327 元
(24x2027(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 15.5%，評等由「買進」調整為「逢低買進」 。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/10/07 收盤價： 283.0 ➢ 26Q2 合併營收 58.8 億元，季增11.1%、年增 23.1%，
毛利率 34.2%、營益率 25.6%，歸屬母公司淨利 12.2
億元、EPS 2.55 元。營收僅略高於研究部原估，惟毛
利率較原估 32.5%高 1.7ppt，為單季獲利優於預期主要
原因。26Q3營收 61.7 億元，季增4.9%、年增27.2%，
續創單季新高。 
➢ 26Q2 毛利率優於預期，主要反映AI與高階測試需求成
長、部分測試價格調升及折舊增幅有限；湖口二廠7月
量產後產能開出速度亦快於原預期，帶動下半年營收與
稼動率提升。AI晶片規格升級使測試項目及時間增加，
加上高階產品占比提高 ，也因此上修 2026 年獲利預估 。 
➢ 湖口二廠， 目前產能利用率約四成； 中興三廠預計26Q4
完工、27Q1開始量產。新增產能主要因應 AI、高階運
算及光通訊測試需求，湖口二廠全年貢獻及中興三廠新
增產能支撐研究部上修 2027 年營收。 
➢ 研究部預估矽格 26F 營收 238.7 億元（年增 21.9%）、
毛利率 33.5%、EPS 9.9元；27F 營收 298 億元（年增
24.9%） 、毛利率35.1%、EPS 13.6元。湖口二廠產能
開出快於預期，加上 AI 及光通訊測試需求成長、測試
價格調升，27 年中興三廠支撐營收與獲利續增；惟新
增設備陸續到位後折舊增加，毛利率擴張幅度可能收
斂，上調目標價 327 元(24x2027(F)PER)，潛在上漲空
間 15.5%，投資評等為「逢低買進」 。

營 運 分 析 
矽格為台灣領先 IC封測廠，攜手台星科布局矽光子封測 
矽格(6257)成立於 1995 年，總部位於新竹，為台灣主要 IC封裝與測試廠商
之一，測試營收占比達90-95%，封裝占比約5-10%。公司提供晶圓測試 （CP，
占 30%）及成品測試（FT，占 70%）服務，並具備WLCSP、RF QFN 等封
裝能力，可提供客戶一站式 turnkey 服務。 
產品應用方面，2026 年 1 至 8 月依應用別，智慧手機占營收約 30%、網通
與物聯網 21%、AI／ASIC／矽光子／高速運算 24%、消費電子與智慧家庭
19%、車用與醫療 6%；公司積極調整產品結構，提升 AI、高速運算與矽光
子等高毛利產品比重，目標中長期 AI 相關營收占比達到與手機業務相當水
準。 
矽格透過子公司台 星科(6335)布局先進封裝領域，台 星科專注晶圓級封裝
（Bumping），目前 N3/N5 製程已量產、N2 完成認證。在矽光子領域，台
星科負責 Bumping 封裝、矽格負責測試，並與客戶共同開發CPO相關產品。
公司自主研發高速高頻 SoC測試設備（MAP），切入矽光子與 RF IC 高階
測試需求，提供客戶客製化測試解決方案。

圖一、矽格(6257) 2026 1~8月產品別營收比重。

個股報告 
26Q2 毛利率優於預期，上修 2026年獲利 
26Q2 合併營收 58.8 億元，季增 11.1%、年增 23.1%，毛利率 34.2%，較
26Q1 增加 2.5ppt，營益率 25.6%，歸屬母公司淨利 12.2 億元、EPS 2.55
元 。 毛利率較研究部原估32.5%高 1.7ppt， 主要受惠AI與高階測試比重提高 、
部分測試價格調升，以及折舊增幅仍低於營收成長。 
2026 年 1至 8 月 AI、CPU/GPU、ASIC及矽光子營收 36.1 億元，年增43%，
占營收 24%，高於去年同期的 20%；記憶體營收 15.9 億元，年增 32%。AI
晶片腳數、功耗及傳輸頻寬提高，使測試項目及單顆測試時間增加，高階測
試營收增幅因而高於晶片出貨量。 
湖口二廠於 7 月開始量產後，產能開出速度快於研究部原先預估，26H2 營
收貢獻亦高於前次模型。研究部上修 2026 年營收由 233.0 億元至 238.7 億
元，毛利率由 32.5%至 33.5%，EPS由 9.46 元至 9.9 元。

AI 晶片測試時間拉長，高階測試產值持續增加 
2026 年 1至 8 月 AI、CPU/GPU、ASIC及矽光子營收 36.1 億元，年增 43%，
營收占比由去年同期 20%提高至 24%；記憶體營收 15.9 億元，年增 32%，
均高於公司整體營收成長。AI晶片腳數、功耗及頻寬持續提高，使測試項目
與單顆測試時間增加，帶動測試產值增幅高於晶片出貨量。 
目前 AI、ASIC及記憶體測試需求維持高檔，公司亦陸續調整部分測試價格。
隨高階測試需求增加，公司 2027 年 AI相關營收占比目標超過三成，仍為主
要成長來源。 
光通訊測試亦持續擴大 ， 目前測試範圍已由EIC延伸至 PIC及 Optical Engine 
(Insertion 1-3)，相關產品除電性測試外，亦需進行光訊號測試及光纖對位，
測試時間與設備要求高於傳統 IC。預期 2027 年公司光通訊測試營收成長優
於 2026。

湖口二廠開始放量，中興三廠接續 2027年擴產 
湖口二廠總建物面積約 8,500 坪，7月開始量產，10月產能已開出約四成，
目前月營收約 1億元；以約八成稼動率視為滿載，滿載月營收約2至 3億元。
依目前爬坡速度，預計 2027 年初達滿載，較研究部原先預估進度快。 
湖口二廠量產後已開始反映於營收。26Q3合併營收 61.7 億元，較 26Q2 增
加 2.9 億元，期間台星科營收下滑，因此營收成長主要來自矽格本體。湖口
二廠產能爬坡速度優於預期，不僅提高 26H2 營收，亦墊高 2027 年的營收
基期。 
中興三廠總樓地板面積約 1.1 萬坪，預計26Q4 完工、27Q1量產，設備將依
客戶需求陸續進駐，新增產能主要投入 AI、高階運算及光通訊測試。研究部
預估 2027 年上半年成長以湖口二廠全年貢獻為主，中興三廠則隨設備到位
逐季增加營收。 
母公司 2026 年資本支出提高至 88億元，主要投入新廠及高階測試設備；矽
格聯測 7 月另追加 20 億元，用於擴充美系 CPU/GPU 客戶測試產能。設備
到位速度將影響 2027 年新增產能開出/營收貢獻節奏。

個股報告

台星科擴充晶圓測試，新增產能主要自 27H2貢獻 
台星科 2026 年上半年營收 27.2 億元，年增 20%，毛利率 27.5%。因應 AI
及 HPC客戶需求 ， 公司2026年資本支出由原規劃 56.48 億元提高至 134.27
億元，主要投入測試及晶圓級封裝相關設備；2027年 Bumping、Testing 及
Assembly產品線均有擴充需求，其中晶圓測試為主要擴產項目之一。先進
封裝方面，55×55mm FCBGA 已進入量產，並持續開發100×100mm 以上大
尺寸 FCBGA 及 2.5D、CoWoS相關製程。由於設備交期較長，新增設備預
計主要自 27H2 陸續到位，相關產能將增加 2027 年下半年營收；另一方面，
大幅增加的資本支出亦將帶動折舊提高，因此台星科對 2027 年集團獲利的
貢獻仍取決於新設備到位及稼動率提升速度，集團毛利率改善仍以 AI、光通
訊等高階測試占比提高及測試價格調整為主。 
圖二、矽格(6257)各季營收及獲利走勢預估

資料來源：Cmoney；兆豐投顧預估

高階測試需求續增，產能擴充效益逐步反映 
研究部預估 2026 年營收 238.7 億元，年增21.9%，毛利率 33.5%、EPS 9.9
元；2027 年營收 298 億元，年增 24.9%，毛利率 35.1%、EPS 13.6元。 
評價方面，矽格 26Q2 獲利優於預期，湖口二廠爬坡速度亦快於原估，加上
AI、高階運算及光通訊測試需求維持成長，研究部上修2026/27F EPS 至 9.9
／13.6 元。前次報告考量AI Connectivity 與矽光子同步推升營收及毛利率，
將評價基準上調至 26x 27F PER，已位於公司歷史評價高檔；本次雖進一步
上修 27F 獲利，但考量 27H2 中興三廠及台星科新增設備陸續到位後折舊增
加，獲利增速與毛利率擴張幅度可能較上半年收斂，因此將評價調整至 24x 
27F PER。以 27F EPS 13.6 元計算，目標價由 301 元上調至 327 元，潛在
上漲空間 15.5%，投資評等由「買進」調整為「逢低買進」 。

## Document 573: 凱基投顧_4966 譜瑞-KY_劉宇程_20261013

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_4966 譜瑞-KY_劉宇程_20261013.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十月 13, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  IC設計 ‧ 台灣

譜瑞-KY 
(4966.TWO /4966 TT )

3Q26 財報預覽：PC/NB 需求較預期疲弱  
重要訊息  
我們根據近期與管理層的交流，更新對公司營運與展望的看法。  
評論及分析  
PC/NB 需求不如預期，下修 4Q26 獲利預估。譜瑞-KY 3Q26 營收達 41.8 億
元，季減 1.6%、年減 4.4%，低於公司財測，主因：(1) DRAM 價格提升抑
制筆電終端需求，使旺季不旺； (2) GPU 供給短缺，進一步壓抑 高階與電競
筆電需求。根據我們的供應鏈訪查，客戶自 7 月下旬起削減下單，8 月降幅
擴大，主要以非 Standard -plus 筆電客戶為主，S tandard -plus 需求則大致正
常，惟亦有所調整。考量記憶體成本壓力較 我們預期嚴峻、客戶拉貨減少，
且記憶體價格尚無緩解跡象，我們下修 4Q26 營收預估至 39.8 億元，季減
4.9%、年增 2.4%。 
PC/NB 需求疲弱，2027 年成長仰賴非出貨量 因素之動能。我們對 PC/NB 需
求轉趨保守，主因我們 認為 2027 年 DRAM 價格將持續上漲，與管理層預期
價格壓力將緩解之看法 不同。不過我們預期成長動能將 來自： (1) TED/tTED
及其他整合式顯示方案取代獨立方案；(2)新一 代 AI PC 平台於 4Q26 進入量
產，單機搭載多顆 retimer，帶動 USB4 retimer 滲透率提升；(3) 車用 DP 
hub 於 4Q26 於電動車平台進入量產；(4) ASIC -like 業務預期將於 2H27 開
始貢獻營收，但在 2028 年前獲利貢獻較不顯著。 此外，由於目前尚無實際
客戶，且 2H27 潛在營收能見度仍 低，我們並未將資料中心 ACC redriver 之
營收貢獻納入財務模型。綜上， 為反映 PC/NB 展望轉弱，我們下修 2027 年
營收預估至 167 億元，年增 2.0%。 
成本提升使 2027 年毛利率回升幅度 有限。我們下修 3Q-4Q26 毛利率預估
至 40.1%與 40.0%，分別季減 0.1 個百分點，反映封測成本與金價上揚，
且各產品與客戶的定價能力不一，成本僅能部分轉嫁。儘管如此，我們預期
封測產能持續移往中國，加上選擇性調漲價格，2H26 毛利率仍可維持在
40%以上。展望 2027 年，考量封測成本壓力持續、成熟製程晶圓代工可能
漲價，以及成本僅能部分轉嫁，我們下調毛利率預估 0.6 個百分點至
40.2%。隨後段產能持續移往中國，我們預期 2027 年毛利率將逐季回升，
但保守看待其改善速度。 
投資建議 
我們下修 2026-27 年 EPS 預估至 30.38 元與 32.22 元，分別年減 12.0%與
年增 6.1%。我們對 PC/NB 展望較先前更為悲觀，但認為中長期主要成長機
會仍在 ASIC-like 業務。我們將目標價由 610 元下調至 515 元，基於 16 倍
2027 年 EPS 預估不變換算得出，維持「持有 」評等。 
投資風險 
PC/NB 需求疲弱；高速傳輸介面 IC競爭加劇。

圖 5：公司概況  圖 6：營收佔比 
成立於 2005 年並於 2011 年上櫃交易的譜瑞 -KY 是混合訊號
IC 設計廠中的佼佼者，產品用於各種顯示與高速介面標準下的
電腦、消費性電子產品與顯示面板中。總部設於加州矽谷而設
計中心則位於上海，譜瑞 -KY 另外設有子公司於南京與首爾，
分公司則開立於香港與台灣。作為 VESA 的共同創辦人與現任
的董事，譜瑞-KY 參與了 DP 與 eDP 的標準開發。

### Page 5

台灣 譜瑞-KY

十月 13, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 5

ESG圖表定義 
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力(公用事業為出售而生產) 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者(太陽能、風能、水能、生
物質能、地熱能)之量 
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2約當排放量 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3) 
譜瑞-KY 首次於 2020 年 CSR 報告書
中揭露該資訊。 
CO2約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸)銷售(百萬元)比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3) 
譜瑞-KY 首次於 2020 年 CSR 報告書
中揭露該資訊。 
廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因(自願或非自願)離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 = (離開之員工/平均員工人數)*100 
- 平均員工人數 = (本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數)/2 
- 本財年末員工數 = 上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練(如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展) 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1天 = 8小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源： Refinitiv ，公司資料， 凱基

## Document 584: 凱基投顧_Takeaway_7776 奧孟亞_陳宣甫_20261014

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_Takeaway_7776 奧孟亞_陳宣甫_20261014.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十月 14, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

法說重點摘要

奧孟亞 
(7776 TT, NT$ 33.15, 未評等)

重點摘要  
1. 奧孟亞以 MIRA/Je 口服胜肽平台切入 GLP-1 口服化市場，透過腸溶
衣、金屬鹽抑制消化酶 與滲透促進劑， ANY002 吸收效率達原廠
Rybelsus 的 3 倍。ANY002 已於 2024 年、2025 年完成兩次國際授
權，首次授權金 462.5 萬美元、分潤 5-15%，南韓授權分潤 20% 
2. ANY004 （口服 Tirzepatide）已於 9M26 完成首次人體 Phase 1，
20mg 、40mg 兩組皆取得正向 PK 數據，下一步為連續 7 天多次給
藥試驗； ANY002 mini 對標原廠新一代 1.5/4/9mg 劑型，預計
4Q26 進入 Pivotal Study ，可望帶來新增授權機會  
3. ANY002 分潤需待各國 Semaglutide 化合物專利到期，美歐日等主
要市場在 2031 年後。我們認為， Wegovy Pill 上市後逾 80%處方來
自新患者，證明口服 GLP-1 為增量市場，而禮來口服小分子進展落
後、尚無口服版猛健樂，使 ANY004 的策略價值明顯高於學名藥性
質的 ANY002  
結論 
奧孟亞的評價重心已由 ANY002 學名藥授權轉向 ANY004 的策略授
權：口服 GLP-1 需求已獲 Wegovy Pill 驗證，禮來尚缺口服胜肽 方案，
而 ANY004 為少數已進入人體階段的口服 Tirzepatide ，且參考禮來與
Camurus 在無人體數據下即支付 1,200 萬美元 upfront ，ANY004 具初
步人體 PK 數據，談判籌碼較佳。 ANY002 分潤到 2031 年後主要市場
專利到期才會放大，而 ANY004 劑量間暴露增幅未達比例、 PK 變異高
於針劑，多次給藥試驗能否達到接近每週一次針劑的藥物濃度，將是
2026 年底至 2027 年授權洽談的關鍵驗證點

MIRA/Je 口服胜 肽平台與技術優勢  
 胜肽藥物選擇性高、脫靶風險低，但口服後會遭胃酸破壞、消化酶
分解，且分子量大難以吸收；FDA 核准的約 75 種胜肽藥物中，系
統性口服僅 Rybelsus 與 Mycapassa 2 種 
 MIRA/Je 三層設計：  
 胃酸端以腸溶衣保護胜肽 結構 
 腸道端加入金屬鹽化合物，降低對應消化酶 活性 
 吸收端加入滲透促進劑，提升腸道吸收  
 專利已申請 66 件、布局逾 50 個市場，採平台加產品配方雙層專
利；已於 Insulin 、Leuprolide 、Teriparatide 驗證可跨分子應用

GLP-1 市場與口服化趨勢  
 2025 年 Tirzepatide 與 Semaglutide 分居全球藥品銷售前兩名，合
計逾 720 億美元，超越 Keytruda ；適應症持續擴及 MASH 、慢性
腎臟病、心血管疾病及阻塞性睡眠呼吸中止症  
 下一代 GLP-1 朝兩個方向發展： 
 療效：由每日一針到每週一針，再到雙靶點、三靶點與雙效療法  
 便利性：口服化與長效化，公司聚焦口服化，鎖定銷售前兩名的
Tirzepatide 與 Semaglutide  
 諾和諾德 Wegovy Pill 於 1M26 在美國上市，前 13 週處方成長快於
猛健樂與針劑版 Wegovy 上市初期，公司指出其為醫藥史上上市最
快的產品 
 Wegovy Pill 美國累計處方逾 300 萬張，其中逾 80%來自未使用過
GLP-1 療法的新患者，口服並非取代針劑而是開啟新增市場；禮來
4M26 推出的口服小分子減重藥成長則相對落後

ANY002 與 ANY002 mini  
 ANY002 以 Semaglutide 為活性成分，吸收效率為原廠 Rybelsus 的
3 倍；美國走學名藥路徑，可透過配方調整使生物利用率與原廠相
當 
 已完成兩次國際授權：  
 2024 年：對象為全球前十大學名藥廠，涵蓋美國、歐盟、印
度、巴西等市場，授權金 462.5 萬美元，分潤 5-15%；已完成
GMP Batch 里程碑，待合作夥伴完成生體相等性試驗（ BE）後申
請上市 
 2025 年：對象為南韓藥廠，僅授權南韓市場，授權金 90 萬美
元，分潤 20% 
 ANY002 與原廠 SNAC 技術不同，僅受 Semaglutide 化合物核心專
利限制；美國、歐洲、日本等主要市場的化合物專利於 2031 年後
到期，印度、巴西等較早到期的市場由合作夥伴爭取先行上市  
 原廠將口服 Semaglutide 糖尿病劑型由 3mg、7mg、14mg 優化為
1.5mg 、4mg 、9mg ，公司已以短時間完成人體驗證並開發出對標
的 ANY002 mini ，預計 4Q26 進入 Pivot al Study  
 ANY002 mini 與原授權劑型不同，既有授權市場亦可重新洽談；公
司自行完成 Pivotal Study 後，可降低合作夥伴的研發與取證負擔，
議價條件可望優於 2024-2025 年的兩次授權

ANY004 Phase 1 數據與後續規劃  
 ANY004 以 Tirzepatide 為活性成分（ GLP-1/GIP 雙重受體促效
劑） ，胺基酸數較多、開發難度較高；原廠禮來目前尚無口服版猛
健樂，也無自有口服技術， ANY004 擬走 505(b)(2) 路徑並以技術授
權商業化

台灣 奧孟亞

十月 14, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 3

 2022 年投入開發，歷經狗、猴子試驗，9 M26 完成首次人體
Phase 1： 
 20mg、 40mg 兩組皆取得正向 PK（藥物動力學）數據，口服的
Cmax 出現快速；40mg 暴露優於 20mg 但增幅未達 2 倍，20mg
可能為後續合適劑量  
 23 名受試者中 12 人出現副作用，多為噁心，無嚴重不良反應，
與治療相關的副作用和其他 GLP-1 藥物相當 
 原廠為每週注射一次、ANY004 為每日口服，現階段無法直接比較
生物利用率；下一步為連續 7 天多次給藥試驗，預期給藥 1 週後
AUC 與 Cmax 明顯高於單次給藥，約 4-5 週可達穩態

授權參考案例與催化劑  
 諾和諾德 2008 年即與 Emisphere 洽談 SNAC 技術合作，upfront 
1,000 萬美元，累計里程碑逾 8,700 萬美元，2020 年以約 18 億美
元收購 Emisphere  
 禮來 2025 年與 Camurus 簽訂長效化技術授權，簽約時尚無人體數
據，upfront 1,200 萬美元， 2026 年再付 500 萬美元；公司認為
ANY004 已具初步人體 PK 數據，可望爭取更佳授權條件 
 ANY002 屬學名藥市場，收入需待各市場實際銷售； ANY004 則以
505(b)(2) 方向尋求策略授權，兩者授權模式不同  
 2026 年底至 2027 年兩大催化劑：ANY002 mini 的 Pivotal Study
帶來新增授權機會； ANY004 人體 PK 數據完成後，與潛在夥伴討
論策略授權，包括合作模式、試驗規劃與下一階段開發方向

QA 
 ANY004 下一階段 20mg 與 40mg 如何選擇？  
 兩種劑量皆可往下做，選擇視合作夥伴希望達到的效果而定  
 下一階段受試人數於最終結果出爐前暫不揭露  
 潛在授權藥廠最重視哪些試驗數據？  
 主要看 AUC 等 PK 指標，但最終仍是談判；授權越早 upfront 越
小、越晚則越大  
 公司認為首次數據結果良好，但仍將完成多次給藥試驗，直接比
較口服連續給藥 1 週與針劑每週一次的差異  
 禮來是否因口服減重競爭而加速尋找口服 Tirzepatide 技術？ 
 Wegovy Pill 成長快速、口服小分子進展有限，公司認為將對禮
來形成很大壓力  
 目前僅奧孟亞將口服 Tirzepatide 推進至人體階段，惟公司不預
測個別藥廠的協商進度  
 ANY004 口服 20mg、 40mg 能否與原廠 2.5mg 針劑直接比較？  
 針劑直接注射進皮下，不需經過口服的降解與消化，因此無法直
接比較劑量；ANY002 可比較是因原廠同樣有口服產品

### Page 4

台灣 奧孟亞

十月 14, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 4

 ANY004 的 PK 變異度高於針劑，但公司認為表現相當不錯 
 公司的收益模式為何？ 
 先收取 upfront 與里程碑金，商業化後依銷售收取分潤；終端售
價與銷售策略由各合作夥伴決定 
 不同授權市場的分潤比例如何決定？  
 不提供逐國明細；越早期、規模越小的市場分潤越低，主要市場
分潤較高，仍視各市場競爭狀況而定  
 未來如何選擇其他胜肽 產品與適應症？  
 GLP-1 帶動胜肽 藥物版圖擴大，2026 年再有 3 款胜肽藥物獲核
准 
 如 ANY005 鎖定的病患為體重僅 5-10 公斤、每天需打針的幼
童，口服化意義大；慢性病患者也常因冷藏保存、自行施打不便
而停針，高齡患者需求尤高

## Document 585: 凱基投顧_Takeaway_7947 補丁科_沈漢軒_20261012

Source PDF: all report pdf/凱基投顧_Takeaway_7947 補丁科_沈漢軒_20261012.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

十月 12, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

訪談重點摘要

補丁科 
(7947 TT, NT$881, 未評等)

焦點內容 
1. 記憶體牆為 AI 效能的主要瓶頸，其中推論對延遲與能耗的要求高於訓
練。硬體算力約每 2 年成長 3 倍，DRAM 頻寬卻只有約 1.6 倍，而
GPU+HBM 採用的 2.5D 封裝，延遲與功耗也相對較高。公司以 3D 堆
疊 near memory 架構切入，有望兼顧接近 SRAM 的延遲，以及遠高於
SRAM 的容量，適合需同時服務百萬級使用者的推論應用。  
2. 公司在 2016 年首次投片即成功，4Gb 產品頻寬達 144GB/s、延遲僅
17ns (HBM 約 40-50ns)，並於 2017 年在 ISSCC 發表。 3D WoW 
hybrid bonding 並非公司獨有，真正的門檻在於客戶、晶圓大廠、南亞
科與公司需四方深度共同開發，公司負責記憶體架構與介面的協同設
計，並以 KGD 技術確保堆疊良率。南亞科持股約 35%，HiBaLL -M 多
層系列目前為 4 層，客戶已要求 8 層。 
3. AI 產品營收占比由 2024 年的 4%提升至 1H26 的約 40%，3Q26 營收
也較 2Q26 成長約 43%。公司自 2025 年起簽訂 SOW，預期明年有客
戶進入量產並開始貢獻營收，其中部分客戶將採 turnkey 模式，量產規
模放大後獲利率有望提高。公司預期 2028-29 年量持續增加， 2 至 3
年內將產生重大影響。  
結論 
補丁科以自有高頻寬低延遲架構結合 3D 堆疊 DRAM，受惠於 AI 推論對低
延遲、低功耗與大容量的需求，以及雲端客戶走向客製化記憶體的趨勢。
短期營收來自 AI 客製化專案與傳統 DRAM 業務；中期成長動能 為明年起
客戶陸續進入量產，帶動客製化專案由設計階段轉向量產階段；長期則有
機會由雲端延伸至邊緣推論，並隨客戶增加與規格收斂推出通用化產品。

營運現況 
 營收：3Q26 季增約 43%，過去六季趨勢向上  
 EPS：1H26 9.35 元，1H26 營收與獲利同步成長  
 產品組合 
 AI 產品營收占比由 2024 年的 4%提升至 1H26 約 40% 
 傳統 DRAM 業務占 1H26 營收約 60%，提供 SoC 廠與系統廠，
並協助客戶客製化  
營運展望 
 明年預期有客戶進入量產並開始貢獻營收，2028 -29 年量持續增加  
 AI 占比持續提升，預期 2至 3年內產生重大影響  
 傳統產品除維持現況外成長有限，AI 為未來重心  
 客戶表示已取得 CSP 大廠訂單，公司目前開發未來產品，產品驗證
成功即可進入後續導入，有別於過去先做產品再推市場

### Page 2

台灣 補丁科

十月 12, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 2

 公司為無廠房的 IP 與設計公司，生產由晶圓大廠 (邏輯 )與南亞科
(DRAM)負責，由客戶向其下單 
成長動能 
 AI 記憶體：記憶體牆與 3D 堆疊解決方案  
 硬體算力約每 2 年成長 3倍，DRAM 頻寬約每 2 年成長 1.6 倍，
二十年累積形成落差  
 現行 GPU+HBM 採 2.5D 封裝，匯流排較長，存取延遲較高、負
載與功耗較高，且台積電 CoWoS 產能大部分由 NVIDIA/AMD 使
用 
 公司採 3D 堆疊 near memory 架構：DRAM 先堆疊，再以 3D 方
式與 CPU/GPU 堆疊 
 HBM 採 micro -bump，公司採 3D WoW hybrid bonding ，pitch
小一個數量級以上，頻寬等於 IO 數乘以頻率，IO 數增加可大幅
提升頻寬，同時達成高頻寬 /低延遲/低功耗 
 AI 推論需求 
 訓練端 HBM 容量遠大於 SRAM，對延遲容忍度尚可；推論端延
遲非常關鍵，已有業者不採 HBM、改採 SRAM 
 客戶以同一模型比較 GPU+HBM 與自家 SRAM 平台，SRAM 平台
約十幾二十秒出答案，GPU+HBM 明顯較慢，使用者無法接受  
 SRAM 容量在百萬級同時使用者的情境下遠遠不足； 3D 堆疊
DRAM 容量遠高於 SRAM，延遲與能耗可做到非常接近 SRAM 
 單一項目未必勝過 SRAM，但整體 AI 效能優於 HBM 與 SRAM 
 HBM 因容量大仍有其價值，公司目標為超越 HBM，產品目前更
適合推論應用  
 技術累積與驗證  
 2014 年與 Intel 合作高頻寬低延遲記憶體計畫，2016 年投片一
次成功，容量 4Gb，頻寬 144GB/s，高於當時 HBM2 
 延遲 17ns，HBM2 乃至今日 HBM 約 40-50ns，成果 2017 年 2
月於 ISSCC 發表 
 2017 年起累積高頻寬低延遲架構，持續為不同客戶客製化，並
具備多種 interface 設計經驗 
 專利全部自行開發，部分已核准，部分申請中  
 競爭優勢 
 邏輯設計能力是 3D 堆疊關鍵：單片良率 80%，不處理的情況下
堆疊 4片剩約 41%，8片剩約 16.7%，10 片剩約 11%，無法量
產，須在底層邏輯設計端克服 
 3D 堆疊接合前可測，接合後 (含溫度循環等熱應力後) 仍可測並可
修復，客戶系統端亦可再測再修復，測試設計須整合進邏輯，與
客戶如同一家公司般共同設計

### Page 3

台灣 補丁科

十月 12, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 3

 與南亞科策略合作，針對客戶高頻寬需求在技術上突破原有框架  
 3D 堆疊技術本身並非稀缺，包括華邦/ 力積電/聯電/台積電/南亞
科均具備，南亞科屏東廠將投入 3D 堆疊與測試 
 差異化在於客製化共同設計能力與團隊 20 多年合作累積的首次
量產成功經驗  
 商業模式與合作條件  
 過去 DRAM 依 JEDEC 標準，各家依標準自行設計；現今 AI 客戶
為超車走客製化，公司從 interface/ 規格定義起即與客戶共同開
發，設計範圍並含處理單元 
 客戶皆為具備足夠規模與資本的 AI 雲端客戶，需使用最先進邏
輯製程(全球目前僅台積電能提供) ，並需 DRAM 端夥伴 
 三大記憶體廠標準品量大獲利高，目前不願大量提供客製化；中
國有其限制，南亞科 DRAM 製程為台灣最先進，現有大客戶都
想找南亞科，也是南亞科積極擴產的原因之一  
 公司角色為與客戶/ 台積電 /南亞科三方共同開發，並協同客戶設
計介面 
 與南亞科的關係  
 2010 年南亞科持股超過 50%，2017 年協議結束後仍維持晶圓
代工合作； 2024 年起重新討論，2025 年因應 AI 記憶體客製
化需求再度投資，目前為最大股東，持股約 35% 
 維持晶圓代工關係，合作模式彈性，可深化合作也可各自發展  
 產品線 
 以 HBLL-RAM 架構為基礎，目前幾乎所有研發量均投入 AI 相關
產品，2017 年架構早期以 2D 方案提供低成本選擇  
 HiBaLL -M 多層系列：多顆 DRAM 以 TSV 堆疊，目前為 4 層，客
戶要求 8層，未來視技術進步而定  
 HiBaLL -S 單層系列：僅一層，相同條件下可提供 10 倍以上頻寬
優勢 
 頻寬>30TBps ，延遲<15ns ，能耗 約 0.2pJ/bit ，HBM 為 2–
3pJ/bit 
 有客戶需求達多個 PB 等級的容量，且要求更低能耗  
 兩者取捨取決於客戶規格需求，並非哪一種必然勝出 
 應用方向 
 目前主要已啟動的 開發案件皆為雲端客戶，為其下一世代擴大服
務做準備 
 邊緣推論客戶已開始洽談，進度較慢屬 AI 應用由雲端先行再落
地邊緣的需求節奏，並非技術問題  
 標準方案無法滿足、對功耗/ 成本有特殊要求的邊緣客戶，也會
來找公司客製化

### Page 4

台灣 補丁科

十月 12, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 4

 傳統 DRAM 業務 
 過去 20 年累積的產品仍在量產，因無自有工廠，以授權及客戶
關係為主 
QA 
 客製化需求增加，是否代表 DRAM 景氣波動減小、價格較為穩定？  
 是，這也是南亞科約 2 至 3 年前決定投入 AI 客製化 DRAM 的原
因，因量體不及三大廠，需走差異化  
 現階段先挑選優質且能長期合作、降低波動性的客戶，客戶成功
則公司與南亞科即成功  
 客戶目標是超越 HBM，通用化並非優點；未來客戶增加、規格
收斂後，有機會推出較通用化的產品  
 與華邦的 CUBE 有何差異？ 
 對方先推出自有標準再尋找客戶導入，並尋求外部協助做標準化
與介面，客製化為輔  
 公司以客戶需求出發，客戶千挑萬選後找來，是希望在製程與設
計上取得特別的東西  
 與愛普的技術路線有何差異？ 
 兩家各有 DRAM 架構，公司認為自家架構更具優勢  
 測試方面， server 等級良率必須測試與修復，公司做法與對方不
同 
 公司深入客戶環境，與客戶緊密共同設計  
 愛普營收規模大於公司，部分來自公司未涉足的業務，目前 AI
需求強勁，各家隨客戶進展成長 
 NVIDIA 若自行在 GPU 上堆疊 SRAM，公司產品是否仍有必要？  
 HBM4 以上也在考慮 3D，NV 可能把部分功能整合進 HBM 的
base die，並與 DRAM 廠討論 3D 整合 
 採客製化或標準化尚不確定，時程比公司稍慢  
 NVIDIA 偏通用型，需兼顧所有應用，CSP 大廠也希望有更多選
擇 
 即使 NVIDIA 做客製化，全球客製化需求也不會只有一家，公司
與南亞科的組合具競爭力  
 3D 堆疊在處理器上，散熱是否會成為更大的問題，是否存在功耗
上限？ 
 功耗是 AI 共同指標，設計初期即規劃，也是客戶採購重要標準  
 公司產品功耗至少為 HBM 的四分之一，目前合作的客戶，整體
系統設計尚未看到散熱成為此方案的限制，這類問題可以解決  
 目前為 4層，客戶要求 8層，設計須隨層數調整

### Page 5

台灣 補丁科

十月 12, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 5

 良率與修復如何看待？  
 設計本身有面積/ 測試等 overhead，4層/8層的目標需分別設定  
 客戶要求目標為 95%，目前至少可做到 80%，有機會更高 
 DRAM 層與層之間也是用 hybrid bonding ？是否提供 2.5D 方案？ 
 DRAM 與 SoC 之間採 hybrid bonding ，多層 DRAM 間以 TSV 互
連，公司提供 DRAM 方案，DRAM 本身仍以 3D 為主 
 與 SoC 的連接方式(含 2.5D)依客戶系統決定，公司 DRAM 端不
需太多改變，部分美國客戶已在測試 2.5D 
 客戶如何選擇商業模式，利潤率如何？  
 預估部分客戶明年進入量產， 量產規模放大後毛利率 可望更高，
發酵時點稍晚  
 客戶選擇與自身資源有關：缺乏後段管理外包商資源者傾向委由
公司處理，有能力者可能自行處理  
 明年量產的晶圓需求如何與晶圓廠洽談？2027 年量產專案使用現
有產線還是新廠？  
 直接關係為公司的客戶，實際為客戶/ 公司/南亞科的三方關係  
 專案啟動前須先與南亞科確認可接受客戶未來需求，各晶圓廠均
需如此洽談；南亞科每年固定詢問需求，近期因新廠開出而增加
確認次數 
 確切數量於下單時確認，大客戶通常提前半年至一年安排並持續
更新；南亞科擴產時會確認可供應量，無法供應的部分會告知一
至兩年後的供應安排  
 2027 年專案現有產線與新廠皆有機會，新廠 2H27 開出 
 合約負債的性質為何？  
 客戶 NRE 的收款方式與財報認列方式不同，財報依投入的時間
與人力成本認列，部分客戶預付大筆現金，記為合約負債 
 人力擴充計畫？  
 目前缺人而非缺錢，正積極招募 
 與客戶合作增加邏輯設計 /實體設計/custom cell 等需求，所需人
才與過去不同。 各階段人力需求不同，前期最多，專案後期釋出
的人力可轉投其他專案，部分部門前後期均需支援  
 客戶洽談先談規格與時程，待人力到位再正式啟動

### Page 6

台灣 補丁科

十月 12, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 6
